## §11 One authority, three projections — storage model + governing principle (owner 2026-08-30)

Governing principle (owner, verbatim intent): **the model proposes;
deterministic Python owns truth.** The LLM never writes a store, never
assigns a canonical id, never becomes a survival dependency. Identity,
admission, the predicate contract, projections, and the control plane are
deterministic; the model is swappable, the guarantees are not.

### §11.0 Contract audit — is the governing design embedded in the control plane? (verified against code 2026-08-30, session 4)

| Claim | Where enforced | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| Lane from durable `documents.byte_length`, ≤300 KB local; enforced at selection AND dispatch | `policy.py` `CLOUD_MIN_BYTES=300_000` clamped floor; `select_lane` + `require_cloud_eligible` raises `CloudBoundaryViolation` immediately before the socket | CONTRACTED |
| Local = GPU window: batched decode, OOM-halving, concurrency 1–4 | `client._infer_batch_call` (500→halve after slot release, budget `record_oom`), `limiter.yaml mlx_local max:4` | CONTRACTED |
| Cloud = rate problem: RPM/TPM buckets before send, AIMD, header sync, breaker | `AdaptiveLimiter` (closed→open→half-open one-probe breaker, `use_headers`), `ollama_cloud` seeds init 3 | CONTRACTED (ceiling raised 16→32 this session — it saturated with ZERO 429s, masking the discoverable limit) |
| Parent unit: model reads child chunks concatenated, never the stored parent row | extract neighborhoods built from `tier='child'` rows; gate `ChunkView`s are children | CONTRACTED |
| Noise never enters an LLM call | `chunks.region_role` (REGION-ROLE-V1) + neighborhood packing filters | CONTRACTED (4.3.15) |
| Output budget scales with input; generation config locked | `output_budget_for`; `GENERATION_CONFIG` = temp 0, rep_penalty 1.15, ctx 400, local `enable_thinking:false`, cloud `reasoning_effort:none` | CONTRACTED |
| Closed predicate enum, exact→alias→RELATED_TO, every fallback recorded | 74-entry `PREDICATE_ALIASES` + patterns; gate counts `predicate_fallbacks`; `predicate_raw`+`predicate_method` persisted on every evidence row AND in facts.provenance | CONTRACTED |
| Off-enum can never become a stored predicate | gate normalizes; `llm_direct` guards `pred not in RELATION_ONTOLOGY` (counted, never expected); verifier `ontology{}` degrades off-enum runs (4.3.16) | CONTRACTED |
| No-think guaranteed twice | engine-layer per lane (above) + `strip_thinking` at the gate | CONTRACTED |
| One control plane: tickets→leases→receipts→census, idempotent, kill-9 resume, lease keeper | `stage_transaction`/receipts, `_lease_keeper`, ticket DAG; census stuck-run class killed by CENSUS-DIRTY-SIGNAL-V2 (4.7.6) | CONTRACTED |
| Coverage accounted, not assumed | EXTRACTION-COVERAGE-V1 dispositions + census promotion barrier (4.3.13) | CONTRACTED |
| Shadow before trust | `llm_shadow` admits nothing; promotion = provider flip (4.7.5) | CONTRACTED |
| Graph hits ground to chunks; identity-admitted facts only | `Fact→Evidence→Chunk` rows written by identity in `llm_direct`; projector reads facts table only | CONTRACTED |
| Deterministic floor: summaries, concepts, procedures model-free | SUMMARY-COMPILER-V1 (4.4.8) + compile_objects stage (11.4) | CONTRACTED |
| Entities carry generation + open vocabulary (de-flattening) | was MISSING — closed by 11.1 | BUILT 2026-08-30 |
| Graph extractions first-class in FAST routing | was MISSING — closed by 11.2 | BUILT 2026-08-30 |
| Exact-name lexical recall (BM25) | was MISSING — 11.3 | BUILT (projection side) 2026-08-30 |

| 11.130 | DOCUMENT-SEMANTIC-INDEX-V1 slice S1 — deterministic ParentSkeleton (plan §7-§10 / §36.1): `shared/polymath_shared/document_profile/parent_skeleton.py` — pure CPU policy (re / Counter / math.log; no summary model, no local LLM, no scikit-learn, no API). `build_parent_skeletons(parents)` returns a `SkeletonManifest` of one `ParentSkeleton` per RETRIEVAL-ELIGIBLE parent (alias P0001 → real parent_id in the manifest; opaque ids never enter the prompt) with a salient EXTRACTIVE sentence (not first-30-words), 3-5 TF×IDF key terms (`idf = log((N+1)/(df+1))+1`), and exact identifiers extracted DETERMINISTICALLY from source (CVE / RFC / dotted version / letter+digit code / zero-padded / acronym, span-masked most-specific-first so no CVE substring leaks) — independent of model hooks (plan §9 / §43.3). Furniture/noise parents are ACCOUNTED FOR as `ExcludedParent`, never dropped; eligibility defers to the single region-role authority `document_region.is_noisy` (no competing furniture policy). `text_hash` / `skeleton_hash` / `manifest_hash` give content identity; `SKELETON_BUILDER_VERSION = parent-skeleton-v1` (versioned per §32). 13 determinism pins incl. 1/80/1000-parent scale (1000 deterministic and < 5 s), identifier fidelity, hash stability, alias stability under reorder, and a static AST purity pin. Additive: no worker / API / persistence / schema / chunk-graph change; nothing consumes it yet (S2 map compiler, S9 map worker next). TOLERANT FORMAT, STRICT IDENTITY: alias drift (`P17`, `MAP \| P0017 \|`, `map\|p3`) resolves by numeric value to a known S1 alias; unknown / ambiguous / invented aliases and empty signatures are rejected and receipted, NEVER attached to a look-alike parent. PARTIAL OUTPUT IS DURABLE (§18.4): 73 of 90 valid lines keep 73, `missing_aliases` is the exact sorted repair set, successful lines never re-run; duplicates keep the first valid record (pinned rule) and receipt the rest. Every `CompiledMap` carries the skeleton's Python `exact_identifiers` verbatim — independent of model hooks (§9). Generic signatures/hooks flagged not fatal (§19.2); an injected instruction inside a MAP line compiles as the signature string — the compiler executes nothing (§30). `MAP_COMPILER_VERSION = map-compiler-v1` (versioned per §32). 15 determinism pins composed with real S1 manifests (40/40, exact partial recovery, identity safety, Unicode fold, deterministic identifiers, injection-as-data, purity). Additive: no worker / API / persistence / schema change (S9 worker calls it, S4 tables store it). | 11.183 | SILICONFLOW-EXTRACTION-LANES-V1 fire-at-max-rate concurrency config (owner 2026-09-08: "configure the concurrency/rate … so it fires at its max rate"). Single-account probes: SiliconFlow exposes **NO rate-limit headers** (`use_headers` is a no-op) and returns **NO 429/Retry-After even at 48 trivial-concurrent** — there is no rate limit to sync against; the ONLY failure mode is ReadTimeout under heavy-request latency saturation. So each lane is `kind: concurrency` (NOT rate — a rate/token bucket would be fiction), ceiling `max: 24` (highest measured-clean/account), seed `init: 12` (climb, don't cold-open at the edge in a slow provider window), `min: 2`, AIMD halves on timeout/503 and climbs +1/8-clean back to 24. **No `family`** → the 3 accounts stay fully independent (a backoff on one never cools the others — the point of 3 separate balances). 3 × 24 = **72 aggregate concurrent graph-extraction slots**. Integrated live qual (client → AdaptiveLimiter → SiliconFlow, N=12): 12/12 ok, 0 errors, limiter climbed 12→15 on clean successes. Guard `test_siliconflow_lanes_are_independent_concurrency` pins kind/family/seed/ceiling + asserts no rate buckets (9 green). | 11.209 | PRODUCTION-CONFORMANCE-AUDIT-V1 — a RE-FIRABLE audit framework (owner mission 2026-09-12): discover what exists, prove what works, prove what is safe to retire. Authority `docs/wiki/plans/PRODUCTION-CONFORMANCE-AUDIT-V1.md`; logic in `shared/polymath_shared/conformance/` (discovery · classify · assess · evidence · attempts · report), orchestration in `scripts/audit_polymath.py`. **Nothing about today's topology is hardcoded** — providers, models, accounts, lanes, workers, routes and tables are rediscovered every run from config + Postgres + the running processes + the code graph; a first run discovered 46 lanes / 7 providers / 15 models / 43 routes / 16 workers / 46 tables without being told any of them. **`NOT_TESTED` and `UNKNOWN` are RED by construction** (`classify.GREEN` holds exactly one state) so absence of evidence can never render as success — the first `--no-spend` run classified 177 components: 69 WORKING_PROVEN, 31 CONFIGURED_IDLE, 14 LEGACY_REQUIRED, 16 RETIRE_CANDIDATE, **47 NOT_TESTED**. **PROVIDER-ATTEMPT-LEDGER-V1 (migration 0056, `llm_provider_attempts`)** closes the gap this framework was built around: measured 2026-09-11, a pMAP run recorded SUCCESS on every batch while issuing **181 HTTP 429s (145 in one 10-min window, an 82% rejection rate)** — in-run cross-lane failover retries a refused lane on the next and only the FINAL outcome reached `document_parent_map_batches`, so provider pressure was invisible to every durable counter, to the control plane, AND to the backfill's own stop conditions. One row per ATTEMPT (lane · provider · model · account ENV NAME · ordinal · limiter admission · dispatch · status · Retry-After · error · latency · success), written at `LLMExtractionClient.complete_one` — the single seam seeing admission, lane, status and Retry-After together; fail-soft (a missing table logs once and the call proceeds) and **no credential is ever stored**. `attempts.reconcile()` reports `PROVIDER_PRESSURE` / `FAILOVER_ATTEMPTS` / `HIDDEN_429` as COUNTS. Retirement follows the existing law (eliminate readers → stop writers → prove no re-mint → remove code → delete state after a rollback window); `--prove-unused` runs the STATIC half and prints that static proof alone is NOT sufficient. Ambiguous references count as READERS deliberately — over-counting readers only delays a retirement, under-counting enables a wrong deletion. 12 agnosticism tests (`test_conformance_agnostic.py`) prove the framework against a SYNTHETIC vendor topology (acme/zeta) with added lanes, renamed models, a removed provider, a disabled lane and reordered fallbacks, plus a negative test that no current vendor token appears in the framework source. `artifacts/audit/` is git-ignored (run artifacts, same rationale as build output). | **SLICES A–C DONE 2026-09-12.** Honest status: L0/L1 proven; **L2–L5 NOT BUILT** — `contract_qualified`/`pipeline_qualified`/`e2e_qualified` emit `NOT_TESTED` rather than a guess; no retirement removal performed (no component yet satisfies the full proof); Control Plane integration and a CI job not built. Root cause: entities store only lowercased surfaces, so the tool protects anything ever seen ALL-CAPS in `mentions` (sound — "US"/"IT"/"WHO" would otherwise be destroyed), but it protected on a SINGLE occurrence, and its justifying comment ("MEASURED: US 49x, IT 6x, WHO 10x") is **STALE**. Re-measured live: `us`→0 mentions, `who`→0, `one`→0, `it`→1, `they`→1, `you`→1 — every protected surface now has exactly ONE all-caps mention, so the rule was protecting styling (a shouted heading), not acronyms. That is exactly why the two gates never converged: the test reported a violation the tool then refused to act on. Fixed with an evidence floor of ≥2 all-caps mentions, stale comment replaced by re-measured numbers, affected surfaces printed before any write; dry-run now finds **3** pronoun-endpoint facts (`it`,`they`,`you`) instead of 0. **Residual ambiguity stated not hidden**: at one occurrence "IT" cannot be distinguished from the pronoun shouted in a heading, so the floor makes the tool WILLING while `--apply` (owner-gated) still performs the write. **Nothing applied — production data unchanged**, and `test_fact_endpoint_eligibility.py` therefore still fails, correctly. Result: **10/10 have live callers — zero removable dead symbols**, so there is no "remove dead code" work outstanding; every symbol is LEGACY_REQUIRED and deleting it would break a live path. **Caught a false positive in my own first hand-run before it became a claim**: excluding all four modules as "own module" reported `note_sparse_fallback` as dead, when it is imported and called by `hybrid.py:40,110,112` — cross-module use INSIDE the legacy set is still use. The gate counts repo-wide for exactly that reason, and the trap is written into its docstring. **STOP WRITERS**: a writer may only be stopped once its readers are gone — stopping one while readers are live is how a table silently empties. Gate reports, for every §12 state probe, whether any has reached zero readers (which is what would make stopping its writer correct): **NONE have**, so all writers MUST keep running, and "writers not stopped" is the CORRECT state rather than outstanding work. The one component that ever reached zero-readers AND zero-writers is `claim_sets`, reported by its own gate and owner-gated at the DROP. **The full retirement sequence is now individually measured**: ELIMINATE READERS (v1_REAL_USER=0 since 2026-09-05) → STOP WRITERS (correctly still running, no probe readerless) → REMOVE CODE (0 dead symbols) → DELETE STATE/SCHEMA (`claim_sets`, BLOCKED_OWNER). Verifier now **16 PASS · 1 BLOCKED_OWNER · 0 FAIL**, RUN 1 → RUN 2 identical, exit 0. | DONE 2026-09-12 — every stage of the retirement law is a measured gate, so "writers not stopped / code not removed" is answered with evidence (correctly-running writers, zero dead symbols) instead of being an unexamined gap. |
| 11.234 | AUTHORITY-GATE-COVERAGE-V1 — a Stop-hook rejection landed a precise, legitimate hit: a 16-gate verifier had been built, but nothing demonstrated those 16 gates ARE the mandatory gates of `POLYMATH_EXECUTION_AUTHORITY_XML_FINALIZED.md`. (1) **"pMAP and existing fast health paths do not regress"** — the authority names these as explicit CONTROLS, so a win on the graph counters is not quietly paid for elsewhere; the verifier measured the graph hot path but had NO pMAP/queue/health timing. (2) **"repo guard passes"** — was run beside the verifier, never as a gate; now one. (3) **"relevant determinism/integration tests pass OR failures are attributed"** — the authority explicitly permits attributed failures, so the gate asserts the failure set is EXACTLY the known one, and breaks BOTH on a new failure and on an attributed one silently vanishing. (4) **WRITE PATH: "one authoritative derivation boundary / deterministic / provider changes cannot silently bypass it"** — now checks the derivation is centralised in `extract_projection.py` and that every artifact WRITE site routes through it. **Self-corrected a misleading gate before it shipped**: the first write-path version scanned the whole repo for `payload->'llm_extraction'->'stats'` reads and printed the hits as "stray" — but those are READS (`census.py`, `semantic_readiness.py`, `extraction_coverage.py`) and §20A explicitly RETAINS the payload "for forensic/detail use"; worse, it printed that alarming list while the verdict ignored it, so it passed anyway. Rescoped to WRITE sites (`receipts.py`, `reconciliation.py`), which is what the clause is actually about. Also added `--fast` (skips the ~6min suite gate, reporting it NOT_TESTED rather than PASS) since making the verifier comprehensive made it slow enough to discourage running. Verifier now **20 gates**. **The new attribution gate immediately earned itself**: its first full run caught a NEW failure this very slice had introduced — `test_conformance_state_census.py::test_claim_sets_has_no_code_reader_or_writer_anywhere`, because `verify_final_state.py` and `retire_claim_sets.py` now NAME `claim_sets` and `reader_writer_census` matches bare mentions. That is the self-reference false positive for the FOURTH time (docstring → filename → governance tool → now a test). Two allow-list entries were added and two MORE files immediately surfaced (`scripts/README.md`, `scripts/scaffold_polymath_v4.py`) — i.e. exactly the whack-a-mole this session had already criticised. So the test was rewritten to assert the ACTUAL invariant name-independently: **no SQL anywhere TOUCHES the table** (`FROM|JOIN|INTO|UPDATE|TABLE claim_sets`), excluding only the migration that created it and the tool that exists to drop it, plus a guard that the census still returns the table so the assertion cannot go vacuous. |

(2) **"citations valid"** — a citation pointing at nothing is worse than no citation, so the gate takes the most recent REAL (non-stub) grounded answer and resolves its cited chunk ids against `chunks`: **9/9 resolve**. | 11.236 | PROVIDER-ATTEMPT-LEDGER-V2 (§15 DURABLE ATTEMPT TELEMETRY) — the ledger's own docstring states its purpose as `lane A 429 · lane B 429 · lane C 200 · **PMAP batch** -> SUCCESS`, "the first two attempts must not disappear". Measured live before touching code: **209 rows, 3 distinct lanes, 0 tagged**, against **13 lanes** with real dispatch activity in durable limiter state. Root cause is one wiring gap, not three symptoms: `record(Attempt(...))` existed ONLY inside `LLMExtractionClient.complete_one` (the single-completion path, taken in practice only by the chat query-compiler lanes), while every batched extraction and every pMAP dispatch goes through `_infer_batch_call`, which recorded nothing. Sharper still — `grep -rn attempt_context` finds exactly ONE caller in the repo, `doc_parent_map_stage_worker.py:232`, so **the only code that TAGS attempts wrapped the only path that recorded none**: the tagging context and the recording call site were on disjoint code paths, which is why 209/209 rows are untagged. Fixed with four `record` calls in `_infer_batch_call` mirroring `complete_one` — limiter-refusal (`limiter_admitted=False, http_dispatched=False`, so a LOCAL refusal stays distinguishable from a provider request), HTTP error (status + `retry_after_s` + `latency_ms` — **the 429 case §15 names**), transport error, success. Recorded as `lane=self.limiter_key` (the lane NAME) not `self.lane` (the transport kind `local`/`cloud`), matching what `complete_one` has always written; recording `self.lane` would have collapsed 13 lanes into 2 and left the two halves of one table disagreeing on what "lane" means. A 500 re-enters the function per half, so each sub-call takes its own `t0` and writes its own row — per ATTEMPT, not per logical call. **Proof at zero provider spend, twice**: 5 mocked branch tests (`test_batch_attempt_telemetry.py`), plus an END-TO-END probe through the real client inside the real `attempt_context`, dispatched at `127.0.0.1:9` (connect refused — no provider contacted, nothing billed) with the row read back out of Postgres: `('selftest-batch-seam','SELFTEST','batch_seam_proof',1,True,True,False,'ConnectError',55,'selftest/none')` — the seam reaches the ledger AND the context tags join. | DONE 2026-09-12 — the last unmeasured REQUIRED FINAL STATE clause; the telemetry was blind on exactly the path its own motivating example describes. | DONE 2026-09-12 — claimed as WIRING-proven, not traffic-proven: all 13 lanes populate on the next real batched run, and `complete_one` remains untagged in practice (a separate, smaller gap, explicitly not claimed closed). |
| 11.237 | PROVIDER-ATTEMPT-LEDGER-V3 (§15) — **corrects 11.236's claim.** That row closed "the batched seam" and called DURABLE ATTEMPT TELEMETRY finished; an AST walk over `LLMExtractionClient` immediately after showed **four** provider-dispatch seams, of which 11.236 covered one. The worse miss was `_extract_prompt` — the single-neighborhood extraction path AND the documented 404 fallback from the batched path — which contains a **retry loop**: 429 on attempt 1, retry, 200 on attempt 2, function returns SUCCESS carrying `attempts=2` as an integer. That is §15's example reproduced inside one function, with the 429 durable nowhere. `complete_batched.run` (the non-extraction batched compilers) was the second miss. **Four fixes:** recording at both seams, per branch, written INSIDE the retry loop with a per-iteration clock so a retried call writes one row per ATTEMPT; error_class vocabulary normalised to `complete_one`'s existing UPPERCASE (`LIMITER_REFUSED`/`HTTP_429`) — 11.236 had introduced a second spelling one field over from the split it had just avoided on `lane`, and the tests asserting the lowercase form failed loudly, which is how it surfaced; `provider` populated (NULL on all 226 rows because no call site set it — now the live base-URL netloc, i.e. where the request WENT, and no credential); `started_at` made honest — the column is `DEFAULT now()` and the row is inserted AFTER the attempt, so a column named started_at held the FINISH time and silently inverted any overlap/pressure analysis; `record` now writes `now() - latency`, which also yields §15's `finished_at` as `started_at + latency_ms`. **The durable part is a gate, not the fix:** two seams went unrecorded for months because nothing enumerated them, so `verify_final_state.py` gains `attempt_ledger_covers_every_provider_seam` (AST: every dispatching function must record; FAIL NAMES the offenders using §15's own term `UNACCOUNTED_ATTEMPT`; exclusions are a PRINTED dict with reasons — `_chat` because both callers record around it and recording twice would double-count, `probe` because it bypasses the limiter by design and a row claiming `limiter_admitted` either way would be false) and `attempt_ledger_shape_matches_s15` (field list present; live rows/lanes/max_ordinal/provider-population printed on PASS as well as FAIL). **Proof: the gate FAILS on the bug it exists for** — `test_attempt_seam_coverage_gate.py` feeds it a doctored client whose new seam dispatches without recording and requires FAIL + the seam's name, then requires PASS once the record is added, so it cannot be satisfied by always failing. Plus `test_the_retry_loop_keeps_BOTH_attempts`: 429(Retry-After:3) then 200 -> exactly 2 rows. 13 tests green. | DONE 2026-09-12 — the lesson is the gate: 11.236 fixed what the diff showed and claimed the clause; enumerating the seams took one AST walk and found three more. | DONE 2026-09-12 — explicitly NOT claimed closed: `probe` (needs a `limiter_bypassed` state = schema change = owner-gated), litellm chat synthesis (dispatches outside the client, out of the gate's stated scope), `response_hash` (never set), and traffic proof (`max_ordinal=1` until a real failover runs). |
| 11.238 | PROVIDER-ATTEMPT-LEDGER-V4 (§15) — V3's seam gate scanned the extraction client and said so; widening the question to "what else dispatches to an external model?" found the chat path. **Answer synthesis was an unrecorded provider seam**: `_litellm_generate` (paid models), `llm_test`, `_ollama_generate` and its `think`-rejected retry `_ollama_stream_plain`, plus `client.probe`. The Ollama pair looked excludable as "local, no quota" — rejected on measurement: **every model in this host's Ollama catalog is `*-cloud`** (12 of 12), so the daemon is local and the inference is not. **They were unrecordable, not merely unrecorded**: 0056 documents `limiter_admitted=false => zero HTTP, zero quota`, and none of these seams passes a lane limiter, so `false` asserts "no spend" about a paid call and `true` asserts an admission that never happened. **Migration 0059** adds `limiter_bypassed` (additive, DEFAULT false, every existing row correctly false); refusals now read as `NOT limiter_admitted AND NOT limiter_bypassed`. A stream has four exits (done, error chunk, non-200, exception) so `_AttemptOutcome` writes exactly ONE row per dispatch on the way out — an abandoned stream records `INCOMPLETE_STREAM`, not a success. **The live probe then exposed two counting bugs no unit test would have**: (1) every attempt of one logical call was `attempt_ordinal=1` — `attempt_context` minted a fresh correlation id per context and counted the ordinal in a contextvar, so a sibling context discarded the increment on token reset; fixed twice over, the correlation id is INHERITED (only the outermost mints) and the ordinal is DERIVED IN SQL (`MAX+1` per correlation), which also survives attempts spanning threads or processes; (2) `failover_attempts = attempts - COUNT(DISTINCT correlation_id)` was inflated by untagged rows, because DISTINCT ignores NULLs — so a seam that merely forgot its context looked exactly like heavy provider failover. Measured live: `attempts=22 logical_calls=6 uncorrelated=14` read as **failover=16**; true value **2**. Now computed over correlated rows only with `uncorrelated_attempts` surfaced separately. **Two more of §15's five named detections implemented**: `LIMITER_BYPASS` (reported as an observation WITH its lanes — probe and synthesis bypass by design, so the fault is a limiter-mediated lane appearing there) and `DARK_ENABLED_LANE` (enabled + credentialled, zero attempts in the window). The seam gate now iterates a list of modules and reports NOT_TESTED — never a vacuous PASS — if one it names cannot be parsed, which is how a static gate usually dies. 25 tests green; live proof at zero spend shows ordinals sequencing 1,2 across sibling contexts with refusals untouched. | DONE 2026-09-12 — three slices in, the pattern is that each fix was found by ENUMERATING rather than by reading the last diff: seams, then modules, then the arithmetic over the rows themselves. | DONE 2026-09-12 — still open and named: `CONFIG/LIVE_MISMATCH` (the third detection), `response_hash` never set, and chat-synthesis rows are wiring-proven not traffic-proven (a real synthesis attempt costs spend; the first chat answer after the bounce populates them). |
| 11.239 | PROVIDER-ATTEMPT-LEDGER-V4-FIX — **11.238 broke chat in production and I reported it as verified.** Wrapping the streaming synthesis generators in an `attempt_context` held open across their `yield`s: a contextvar token is valid only in the Context that created it, Starlette resumes a sync streaming generator in a different one, and `__exit__` raised `<Token ...> was created in a different Context`. Every `/chat` answer ended as a stream error for the ~14 minutes between the two commits. **It stayed invisible because the live test hid it**: `test_chat_synthesis.py` ends its stream reader with `pytest.skip("stream error (LLM lane, not the contract under test)")`, which is right for a provider fault and wrong for ours — the suite read **10 passed, 3 skipped, exit 0** and I took that as proof the restructured generators worked live. It surfaced only on re-running with `-rs` to see WHY three tests skipped. **Fixed structurally first**: no attempt context spans a yield — `_AttemptOutcome` captures the ambient correlation id on entry and opens a context only around the `record()` call, `_litellm_generate` does the same via a `_rec_attempt` helper, so grouping survives without contextvar state crossing a suspension point; `attempt_context.__exit__` additionally restores by VALUE when the token is foreign, because diagnostics around a user-visible stream must degrade rather than throw. **And the masking, which is the more valuable fix**: the live test now skips only on error codes that NAME a provider condition and `pytest.fail`s on anything else. Result after the fix and a bounce: **13 passed, 0 skipped** (was 10/3). **First traffic proof for 11.238's chat seam arrived with it** — six genuine synthesis attempts on `chat_synth:anthropic` / `anthropic/deepseek-v4-flash-0731`, latencies 7.0s to 59.0s, all previously invisible. | DONE 2026-09-12 — the lesson is not "be careful with contextvars": a SKIP is not a PASS, and a test that classifies an unknown error as someone else's problem will hide the next one too. | DONE 2026-09-12 — only this test's skip branch was audited; other live tests may carry the same shape. |
| 11.240 | SKIP-MASKING-AUDIT-V1 — 11.239 fixed the skip that hid a production break; this answers the question it left: how many others are shaped like that? **49 `pytest.skip` calls audited**; most are honest environment gates whose condition is a refused connection. The filter was "could this condition be caused by our own code?" and it selected three. (1) **`test_runtime_config_contract.py` could not fail**: raise -> skip, return -> assert, so the only failure mode of "validate_startup passes against the real deployment" was unreachable — and `POSTGRES_AUTH_FAILED`, the code it most exists to catch, was among the skipped; now only `POSTGRES_CONFIG_MISSING`/`POSTGRES_UNREACHABLE`/`POSTGRES_DRIVER_MISSING` may skip. (2) **`test_retrieve_plan_capabilities.py` skipped on a guess** — any `errors` payload was reported as "corpus not query_ready" when the real condition was `QUERY_SCOPE_UNKNOWN` for a HARDCODED corpus id absent from this deployment; `_ready_corpus()` now asks `/corpora` and names the true reason, and the guard matches ERROR CODES — the substring version matched `corpus_id`, which appears in every error as a FIELD NAME, so **the guard admitted everything**, the same match-the-mention bug the conformance censuses had. Both tests in that file now **PASS instead of skipping**: they had been silently not running. (3) **`test_medic.py` labelled instead of asking** — a broad `except` around `SELECT 1 FROM medic_actions` reported any connection drop or permission problem as "migration 0053 not applied"; now `to_regclass`. **Durable part**: the verifier's suite gate reports the **SKIP COUNT** on PASS as well as FAIL — a rising skip count is the visible shape of coverage quietly leaving, and it was invisible. | DONE 2026-09-12 — the audit recovered two tests that had been permanently skipping, which is the strongest evidence the shape matters. | DONE 2026-09-12 — scope was `tests/`; `scripts/` has its own skip-like early returns, not reviewed. A lint rule banning skip-inside-except was rejected: the legitimate uses have exactly that shape. |
| 11.241 | PROVIDER-ATTEMPT-LEDGER-V5 (§15) — closes the two gaps 11.238/11.239 named as explicitly NOT done. **`response_hash`**, NULL on every row since the ledger landed, is now set at all seven success seams; it is what makes two attempts comparable without keeping the response, so a lane returning the SAME body for different prompts (stuck model, cached edge, error page served with HTTP 200) becomes obvious where status codes show nothing. Streamed answers are hashed INCREMENTALLY as pieces arrive — the synthesis path streams precisely so it need not hold the answer, and a diagnostic must not change that — and an EMPTY answer records NULL rather than `sha256("")`, which would have made every empty answer look identical to every other. **`CONFIG/LIVE_MISMATCH`**, the fifth and last of §15's named detections, is implemented: config is a claim about what the system WILL do and the ledger records what it DID, so it reports a lane that dispatched but is absent from the registry, and a lane whose configured model is not the model its attempts carried (a stage pin that never took effect, or a silent provider substitution). **All five detections now produce real findings live**: FAILOVER_ATTEMPTS 2 · LIMITER_BYPASS 24 (chat_synth, by design) · DARK_ENABLED_LANE **31 enabled, credentialled lanes with zero attempts in 24h** · CONFIG/LIVE_MISMATCH — whose first finding was MY OWN probe lanes, kept rather than excluded, because suppressing the first true thing a new detector finds is how detectors become decoration. 19 tests green. | DONE 2026-09-12 — §15 is now implemented in full: every field it lists is written, every detection it names exists, and every provider seam reaches the ledger. | DONE 2026-09-12 — surfaced but NOT diagnosed: the 31 dark lanes. `cost` remains unrecorded (§15 lists it as "if available"). |
| 11.242 | DARK-LANE-DIAGNOSIS-V1 (§15) — 11.241 surfaced "31 enabled, credentialled lanes made no attempt in 24h" and explicitly did not diagnose it. **Diagnosed: all 31 belong to functions that were not running** (GRAPH_EXTRACTION 18, PMAP 6, parent_enrichment 4, DOCUMENT_PROFILE 2) — no ingestion has run on this host in the window, 265 stage tickets sit pending and unleased, so those lanes are idle because there is no work. One fact reported 31 times as though it were 31 problems; a detector that fires on "the pipeline is idle" trains its reader to ignore it. **Refinement 1**: a lane is dark only if its FUNCTION was working; idle functions are reported as CONTEXT, never as a finding. Output went from 31 lines of noise to one specific claim — `compiler_alibaba_deepseek [CHAT, 3 siblings working]`. **Then I chased that one and it is not a defect either**: the lane is in the stage pin, present in `cloud_endpoints()`, and reaches the roster, so I TESTED the rotation instead of reasoning about it — over 4000 distinct session keys `_compiler_attempt_order` is uniform (home 25.5 / 25.4 / 25.1 / 24.0 %, appears in the 3-attempt order ~75% each). The window simply held **28 logical calls** from a handful of reused session keys, and a lane absent from 28 draws over 4 lanes is unremarkable. **Refinement 2**: the finding now STATES its sample size (`28 logical call(s), 356 attempt(s) — with few calls, absence is expected, not evidence`) so the next reader does not repeat the hour. **Also corrected a stale note my own work invalidated the same day**: `conformance/assess.py` documented "as of 2026-09-12 the ledger is populated only for the CHAT-compiler lanes ... a separate, pre-existing gap, not something this slice fixes" — 11.236-11.238 closed exactly that gap hours later. 35 tests green. | DONE 2026-09-12 — the detector was right to ask and wrong to shout; both refinements came from acting on its own first output rather than from review. | DONE 2026-09-12 — session-key diversity in the observed traffic is low by construction (28 logical calls, few keys), so any real neglected-lane claim needs a window with real user traffic. `cost` remains unrecorded. |
| 11.243 | VERIFIER-VACUITY-AUDIT-V1 (§18) — 11.240 found TESTS that hid failures behind skips; the same question applies with more force to the one command that certifies the whole REQUIRED FINAL STATE. **Worst shape checked first and clean**: an AST walk over every `gate(...)` call confirms no gate reports PASS from inside an exception handler — all 13 gate calls within `except` blocks report NOT_TESTED (12) or FAIL (1), so the verifier cannot certify an error. **Subtler shape — a verdict an EMPTY input satisfies** — found three: `retrieval_truthful_mode` tested that requested and executed modes do not CONFLICT, which a response carrying **no mode metadata at all** satisfied (it could not tell "reported the right mode" from "reported nothing"); `outbox_corpus_scoped` tested "no Seq Scan on outbox_events", trivially true of a plan that never touched the table — a corpus with **zero documents** certified an index path it never used; `hot_path_no_toast_detoast` tested "the plan mentions no payload/TOAST", trivially true of an **empty plan**. Each now demands positive evidence: the executed mode must be PRESENT and equal with all three modes answering; the outbox plan must mention `outbox_events` and the corpus must have doc_ids; the graph plan must carry an `Execution Time` line. All three still PASS live — they were reporting truthfully, they simply could not have detected the vacuous case. 6 tests feed each gate its vacuous input and require FAIL, then a real input and require PASS, so the strictness cannot be met by failing everything; the AST invariant is pinned so a future `gate(..., PASS)` inside an `except` fails the suite. | DONE 2026-09-12 — "they pass now so it does not matter" is the wrong test: a gate's value is what it does when the system is NOT healthy. | DONE 2026-09-12 — scope was `verify_final_state.py`; `retire_*.py` and `repo_guard.py` were not examined for the same shape. |
| 11.244 | RETIREMENT-PROOF-VACUITY-V1 (§1, §18) — 11.243 asked whether the verifier could pass on nothing; the same question asked of the script that authorises `DROP TABLE` had worse answers. **`retire_claim_sets.py` had two ways a MISSING measurement read as a PERMISSIVE one.** (1) `never_written = (not present_in_stats) or (ins==0 and upd==0 and del==0)` — a table leaves `pg_stat_user_tables` after `pg_stat_reset()`, on a replica, or for an uncovered schema, so "no evidence of writes" was read as "there were none", on the one verdict that authorises an irreversible DROP. Now returns UNPROVEN and refuses, naming the three causes. (2) The code census ran `git grep` and read zero hits as "nothing references this table" — a pattern the local grep cannot parse, or a wrong cwd, gives the identical empty result. There is now a **positive control**: the same pattern shape must find `chunks`, which the repo certainly references; if it does not, the census is broken, its empty result proves nothing, and the script exits. **`retire_pronoun_facts.py` has the MIRROR risk** — an empty result there does not block a deletion, it ENABLES a larger one, because `acronymic` is the set protecting "US"/"IT"/"WHO" from being deleted as pronouns. It now refuses when `mentions` has rows but the protection set is empty ("a broken protection query, not a corpus without acronyms") and when `mentions` is empty at all — checked BEFORE the `--apply` branch, since a check after it protects nothing. 7 tests, including that the positive control really is present in this repo (a drifted control is not a control) and that the pronoun refusal PRECEDES `--apply`, asserted on source order. Live verdict unchanged: `claim_sets` rows 0, lifetime 0/0/0, references NONE, DEAD_PROVEN still holds. | DONE 2026-09-12 — "it found the right answer today" is reasoning from the outcome; the control is what makes that checkable on the day it stops. | DONE 2026-09-12 — neither script's ROLLBACK path is exercised end to end; the restore DDL is complete-by-inspection for an empty table, which is weaker than a test. |
| 11.245 | AUTHORITY-GATE-RECOVERAGE-V1 (§23, §22, §20A, §17) — **I reported "25 PASS · 1 BLOCKED_OWNER" without re-reading the authority in this session.** Re-reading it (25 `<section>` blocks + 29 other top-level blocks, mapped clause by clause) found **three mandatory clauses with no gate at all, and a fourth gate that was greener than reality**. (1) `frontend_public_url_serves_v2` passed on `302 -> /v2/` from the site ROOT; probing `/v2/` itself — which no gate did — returns **HTTP 401 `www-authenticate: Basic realm="restricted"`**, confirmed in a browser (blank page, one 401 in console). The census's first version flagged a REPORT.md and a JSON manifest for merely NAMING the symbol — the match-the-mention bug fixed twice already this session — and now matches an import line. (4) Re-fire was performed repeatedly but never PROVEN: migration **0060** adds `verification_runs`, the verifier appends its per-gate verdicts each run, and `verification_refires_identically` compares against the last clean run at the same commit — exposing a verification that is not reproducible AND a re-fire that never happened. NOT_TESTED on a first fire, never PASS. | DONE 2026-09-12 — the lesson is the one the Stop hook named: a gate list derived from the authority MONTHS or HOURS ago is not the same as checking it against the authority now. | DONE 2026-09-12 — the real URL's functional verification stays BLOCKED_OWNER until the owner supplies the basic-auth credential; browser-only checks are hand-verified and recorded, not re-firable without a browser driver. |

The 9 themes each redefine the full token set, but `app.css` had **11 rules with BAKED-IN colors** tuned for the dark default that ignored the active palette: `table tr:hover` = `#11151a` near-black (the "black hover", unreadable on light themes), banner TEXT `#f0d9a0/#f3b3b0/#9be0ac` (light text → ~1.5:1 on light themes' light tint), `banner--info` bg `#0f1b2b` (dark box in a light UI), `btn--primary` border/hover `#2f5c96/#234a78` (fixed blue clashing on espresso/rose/solar/paper), and pill/delete borders as fixed darks. **All replaced with theme-derived values**: text on any status surface → `var(--fg)` (status carried by the coloured border + a per-theme color-mix tint), row hover → `color-mix(var(--accent) 10%, var(--bg))`, borders → `color-mix` of the relevant status/accent into `--bg`/`--line`. **Proof**: build clean; computed WCAG contrast of --fg on the fixed surfaces is **8.8:1–14.8:1** across slate/paper/champagne/obsidian (past AAA 7:1; the replaced light-on-light was ~1.5:1); grep confirms the only literal hex left in app.css are the :root default tokens + shadows. | DONE 2026-09-12 — the theme tokens were fine; the bug was rules that bypassed them, now all theme-derived. | DONE 2026-09-12 — `--fg-faint` labels sit ~2.5-3:1 on the warmest light themes by design (faint); raise if a real label reads too weak. |
